# aterm-release: spec recovery from its unit tests

Each §1 row is one property of `crates/aterm-release` that its tests sample, stated from the code.
Once Trust/Clean proves a spec about the compiled function, the tests it subsumes are deleted. §2
maps each test to its specs, §3 summarizes coverage, §4 lists the tests that are not spec samples,
and §5 lists implied specs that look wrong or disagree with the code.

> Status 2026-10-17: the rows naming 6 more deleted tests and 6 more specs whose functions are

> rustflags readers) are gone too. The §1 kind, gap and tier tables count the 362 specs left.
> Other counts in this file were taken before.

> Status 2026-10-16: this is the crate's migration worklist. Each spec below is proved through
> Trust/Clean about the compiled Rust function; then the tests it subsumes are deleted. Nothing has
> been proved or deleted yet. The non-spec tests in §4 carry their own actions.
>
> **Blocked: `exact-delete-absence`** (2026-10-16), and with it every spec in this crate, by gap 9 at
> crate scope. On Trust seal `150efe4a` the evidence-grade `targo trust check -p aterm-release`
> refuses the whole unit before verifying anything: `error: verified Targo refuses unit aterm-release
> … in-process proc-macro dependencies [aterm-serde-derive …::aterm_serde_derive] share rustc's argv,
> memory, stderr, and artifact filesystem and can forge compiler-message/TRUSTJSON proof transport or
> mutate authenticated outputs. Evidence-grade verification currently enforces a no-proc-macro TCB
> boundary`; report.json then has no row for any fn (`transport:missing-json`, COULD NOT RUN). The
> contract itself is ready: with `trust_contract_fn!`/`verify_island!` local to `publish.rs`
> (`tests/it` mounts that file without `main.rs`) and the arms taking `$vis:vis const fn`, the clause
> `ensures result == (preexisting_absence_is_converged || delete_attempted) by
> exact_delete_absence_is_disjunction` with the Clean theorem `∀ (a b : Bool),
> trust_import_aterm_release__publish__exact_delete_absence_is_converged a b = (a || b)` (proof:
> `simp only [<the import>]`) is accepted on the advisory lane (`--allow-l0-gaps`): the fn's row is 3/3
> proved, postcondition `clean-kernel-citation`, `Certified`, KernelRechecked. That lane forfeits the
> evidence-grade TCB boundary, so it does not count; the code is unchanged and no test is deleted.
> Note the import name carries the module path (`__publish__`); the bare
> `trust_import_aterm_release__<fn>` is rejected as `AutoBoundHeadOverApplied`. Lifting it needs the
> serde derives expanded out of this crate's dependency graph, or a seal that admits proc macros.

Source: `crates/aterm-release` (read only). Every `#[test]` in `build.rs`, `src/**` and `tests/**`
was read together with the code it reaches, in three chunks (347 + 348 + 185 = 880). The 880 test
names were then matched mechanically against the files: every spec cites only tests that exist, and
every test is either cited by a spec or listed in §4. Nothing was compiled or run. The claims come
from reading the code.

Result: **371 specs**, covering **849 tests**. **30 tests are not spec samples** (§4):
7 delete, 21 move-to-bench, 2 becomes-spec-after-fix.

The chunks overlapped on nine properties seen from different files; each was merged into one spec
(`rel-expected-embedded-pin`, `rel-channel-asset-set`, `rel-changelog-roll`, `rel-devid-preflight`,
`rs-scan-release-page`, `journal-roundtrip-valid`, `remeasure-fold-own-key`, `judge-floor-rule`,
`rs-cli-parse-grammar`). Spec ids keep their chunk prefix (`rel-` for the library modules read in
chunk 1, unprefixed for the Trust-lane and integration modules of chunk 2, `fx-/rf-/rl-/rs-/sc-/tg-/ss-/sn-/tf-`
for the integration files of chunk 3) so a reader can trace each back.

## 0. Notation used in the statements

Paths. A test is `<path>::<test fn>`, `<path>` relative to `crates/aterm-release` (`src/…`,
`tests/it/…`). A function is `<module or file>::<item>` in this crate unless another crate prefixes
it (`aterm_update_core::…`, `aterm_models_release::…`, `atpkg_keys::…`) or a `crates/<crate>/src/…`
path names its file.

Strings and digests.
- `lines(s)` = `str::lines(s)`; `trim` = `str::trim`; `chars(s)` counts Unicode scalar values.
- `GL(s)` = `github_length(s)`, the number of UTF-16 code units in s. `L` = `GITHUB_RELEASE_BODY_LIMIT` = 125 000.
- `hex40(s)`: 40 bytes in `[0-9a-f]`. `hex64(s)` = `canonical_sha256(s)`: 64 bytes in `[0-9a-f]`.
  `HEX40U(s)`: 40 bytes in `[0-9A-F]`.
- "Err naming X" / "Err 'X'": the error text contains X; other wording is not part of the spec.
- `canonical X.Y.Z`: three decimal u64 components, no leading zeros, no prefix or suffix.
  `version_triple(v)` = `Some((a,b,c))` iff v splits on '.' into exactly three parts that parse as u64.

Effects and traces.
- `Calls(r)`: the ordered argv vectors (program, then args) an injected runner, fake git, fake world
  or fake rustup received during one call. `Env`, `Dir`: a built `Command`'s env overrides (set and
  removed) and `current_dir`. A spec whose `rust_features` says "Command builder" fixes program,
  args, Env and Dir, and nothing else.
- `Fs`, `Fs'`: file-system state before and after a call. An effect spec waits on gap 8.
- A *trace* (`apple-hooks-trace`, `notarize-and-package-order`) is the ordered list of calls made on
  an injected `AppleTools`/`Packager`; the spec quantifies over every implementation and every result.

Release flow (`release.rs::run_with`). A world W is the `Backend` state plus the release dir R =
`B.release_dir()` (`<git common dir>/aterm-release`, holding `JOURNAL_FILE`, `RUNNING_FILE` and the
`RUNNING_CHILD_FILE` notes). `run(W, o)` gives (W', `Outcome{code, last}`, Out = the `B.say` lines,
Calls = the `Backend` methods invoked). The mutating set is M = {sync, bump, carry, move_to, commit_gates
(not dry), stage, reserve, build, stage_fixtures, verify, publish, sign, source_publication, cut,
after_cut, land_fixtures, ask, resolve, withdraw, reexec}, where stage, build, stage_fixtures and verify
are the qualify graph's `Branches` (export, build, verify of a half) or `Backend::stage`, and publish
and sign are step 7's sides (`Branches::source`, `Branches::sign`, the call `cut --sign`); `Calls|M` is
Calls restricted to M. Steps are `Step::ALL` = [1 checks, 2 sync, 3 commit, 4 qualify, 5 bundle, 6 ready,
7 publish, 8 cut, 9 after-cut, 10 check]; `s.at()` = "n/10 name". `Final` = `last` ∈ RELEASED … | PREPARED … |
DRY RUN … | REFUSED at s: why — public. remedy | INTERRUPTED at s (by SIGx) — public. remedy. Its
code is 0 for RELEASED, PREPARED and a would-go-ahead DRY RUN, 1 for a decisive REFUSED, 3 for a
COULD NOT RUN refusal, 130 for INTERRUPTED. `Tail` is origin's ledger tail as `B.origin_tail`
reports it.

Cut journal (`publish::Journal`). `valid(j)` ⇔ `Journal::validate` accepts j: `format ==
JOURNAL_FORMAT`; a canonical version; a 40- or 64-hex commit; `done` an exact prefix of `STEPS` =
[lock, sign, selfcheck, tag, publish, unlock]; a Linux handoff, if any, that `validate_identity`
accepts for that version, build and commit; `min_build ≤ build_number`; until sign is done, a
verified bundle (64-hex `artifact` and `macho_sha256`); a canonical `signature_pubkey` iff
`signature_required`; `manifest_signed` ⇒ `signature_required`, and `signature_required` ∧ sign
done ⇒ `manifest_signed`; a nonzero release id, present once publish is done and only with
`release_intent`; upload intents unique `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+` names, only with that bound release.
`finished(h)` ⇔ "unlock" ∈ h.done.

Lease (`publish.rs`). `RELEASE_LEASE_REF` is the remote cut lock, holding its owner's oid;
`PUBLISHER_FENCE_REF` holds the per-invocation fencing token. Releasing either returns a
`LeaseRelease` or `FenceRelease` ∈ {Released, AlreadyAbsent, AlreadySuperseded}.

Site (`site/mod.rs`). `sync(opts, env, World, out)` → `Result<Outcome, Failure>`; World is the
injected `site::World` (GitHub, download, live site, engine build, wasm-opt, deploy through Firebase
Hosting's REST API in `site::hosting`, launch). `Owned` is `OWNED_FILES` plus `OWNED_GLOB` (the
engine's wasm pair); `Owed` is the marker `.git/<OWED_MARK>`.

Snapshot (`snapshot.rs`). `Tree(repo, rev)` is rev's recursive tree read from objects, each
submodule at its gitlink. A *take* is the materialized directory under the held cache.

Grid (`publish.rs`). `VALUE_COL` = 13, `LABEL_MAX` = 10; a token is a maximal run of non-space chars.

Refinement. A refinement spec binds production transitions to a model: a `ty_model!` in
`aterm-models-release` (`ReleaseChannelFloor`, `ReleaseDurablePostIntent`, …), the channel-head
model `aterm_spec::derive` builds, or the abstract world the statement defines. Every observed
state, projected onto the model's variables, is reachable by an enabled healthy action, and a kept
mutant's is not.

Gaps. `blocking_gaps` numbers are the rows of §7 of `docs/AUDIT-trust-for-test-migration-2026-10-15.md`:
0 seal, 1 non-scalar carriers, 2 `&self`/`&mut self` views, 3 E6 body widening, 4 callee-ensures
composition, 5 quantified clauses, 7 loops, 8 effects, 9 proc-macro boundary (serde/toml),
10 model→code refinement, 11 generics, 12 cross-crate citation, 13 closures/dyn/higher-order. Gap 0
blocks every spec and is implied; the column lists each spec's own gaps beyond it.

none), `rust_features` (what the proof must handle), `blocking_gaps`.

judge-floor-rule	contract	trust_facts::judge, trust_facts::credit, trust_facts::Pace::starved, trust_facts::floor_run, trust_facts::backstops_rewritten, trust_facts::write_floor_files, trust_facts::measure_again, trust_facts::floor_of, trust_facts::trustd_remedy	∀ facts f, ratchet r: judge(f, r, ..., Update::NONE).word = "PASS" iff every ratchet row (c, floor, total, prover[, reason]) has measured proved(c) ≥ floor (the judge alone ignores up-only and prover-column rules; `trust judge` adds them through lowered_since_measured). Per crate c with Result (p, t) and floor F(c) (any prover's row is held): PASS iff p ≥ floor; FAIL when there is no floor (until --update-ratchet records one), the floor is not a count, or a hand-lowered row has t above its total. A run is starved when its pace's CPU/wall < 0.8 or its mean load > its cores. Its credit = its backstopped obligations not backstopped in the floor's run (the multiset difference of their keys against c's BACKSTOPS rows; without this run's record, lapsed + rss − |those rows|) + its cancelled-proof-check lines beyond the floor's run's count; when BACKSTOPS has no head row for c (floor_run = None) the floor's run is unknown, so every stop and cancelled check is credited and none is called new. The prover's spread A = spread(floor) = ⌊floor × SPREAD_PER_CENT / 100⌋ (2%, the run-to-run difference measured on one tree under one prover) when the row's prover column is empty or this run's prover, else 0. p < floor is UNDECIDED (exit 2, in neither total, named for re-measure) if the shortfall ≤ the trustd refusals + (the credit if starved, else 0); or the run is starved, its credit > 0 and the shortfall ≤ its credit + A (NOT-VERIFIED(clock,<F,credit): the note names the pace, what is credited and what the spread takes); or it names no obligation lost (credit = 0, or floor_run = None, where no stop can be called new) and the shortfall ≤ A (NOT-VERIFIED(spread,<F,shortfall), a WITHIN SPREAD note), at any load; else FAIL, with a LOST note naming the run's credited stops when it had stops or a starved run's credit, and a BEYOND SPREAD note when the shortfall > A > 0. A run that names a lost obligation (credit > 0 against a kept floor run) and is not starved is FAIL within the spread too. What the floor's run also had earns nothing at any load. --update-ratchet sets or raises no floor from a starved run whose credit > 0, nor from one a backstop stopped that kept no record; a floor it writes rewrites c's BACKSTOPS rows to a head row with the run's cancelled count and then its backstopped set, written with the ratchet (each staged beside itself, then moved, so a failed write leaves both as they were). --update-ratchet never lowers a same-prover floor; lowers another prover's only with a column-5 reason; records first floors under the measuring prover; raises to the high-water mark; rewrites a corrected total with the floor unchanged. A check targo would not start is NOT-VERIFIED(trustd) (COULD NOT RUN), never GATE-BROKE; so is one whose trustd_lost is set (the keeper saw trustd end its grant while it ran) and that measured nothing (transport, ended ≠ '', or no Summary), its note naming what then served the socket, never NOT-VERIFIED(rc=…) or NOT-VERIFIED(incomplete); one that finished before trustd went is scored. One whose rc is 3 (targo-trust's COULD NOT RUN exit) with could_not_run ≠ '', no Summary and no refused path is NOT-VERIFIED(targo) (COULD NOT RUN), its note quoting targo's words; at any other rc, or with a refused path, it is the FAIL it was, and with a Summary it is scored.	src/trust_facts.rs::the_judge_holds_floors_as_the_lane_did, src/trust_facts.rs::a_lapsed_shortfall_is_undecided_never_a_pass, src/trust_facts.rs::a_cancelled_proof_check_is_credited_only_in_a_starved_run, src/trust_facts.rs::a_shortfall_is_the_clocks_only_in_a_starved_run_and_only_for_a_new_stop, src/trust_facts.rs::a_shortfall_within_the_provers_spread_is_undecided_and_a_named_loss_is_not, src/trust_facts.rs::a_check_whose_trustd_went_away_is_could_not_run_and_never_the_codes, src/trust_facts.rs::targo_trusts_own_could_not_run_is_undecided_and_never_the_codes, src/trust_facts.rs::an_rss_stop_is_credited_as_a_clock_stop_is, src/trust_facts.rs::a_starved_run_sets_no_floor_the_machine_can_be_credited_with, src/trust_facts.rs::a_refusal_covers_only_itself_in_a_run_that_was_not_starved, src/trust_facts.rs::a_pace_is_starved_below_four_fifths_of_its_wall_or_above_the_cores, src/trust_facts.rs::the_floors_backstops_keep_each_crates_own_rows, src/trust_facts.rs::the_floor_files_are_written_together_or_not_at_all, src/trust_facts.rs::a_check_targo_would_not_start_is_undecided, src/trust_facts.rs::the_fences_still_fire_from_stored_facts, tests/it/trust_facts_model.rs::the_unchecked_reuse_reader_is_refused, tests/it/trust_facts_model.rs::the_retired_floor_excuses_are_refused	BTreeMap floors, TSV rewriting, u64 compare	1,3,5,7

Keeper::start(s) holds a RESERVE grant (held()) only on this build's trustd: its worker tries s every 2 s until stopped, registers each connection (Kept::name) before it waits on it, sends RESERVE only after IDENTITY says Ours, and held() is true only from a GRANTED reply to that connection's end; any other IDENTITY answer (another build's, `ERR busy` or none) is written to the seen file instead, and no RESERVE is sent. If the root's serial cannot be read, or the record cannot be rewritten naming root = (pid, serial), or open (one '\n' down the gate's pipe) fails, start kills the root, sweeps its mark (end_marked, Act::End, the root held), reaps it, removes the record iff the sweep is spent and either it left Nothing or the slot's stamp was written, and is Err ('could not be identified' for the first); otherwise it returns the RealCheck. no other row is the check's. proven_session(L, root, held, still) = root iff root is Some and held, or a row carrying the mark with sid = root still runs (still), else None; Live::session asks it with the held pid or the mark's root, still = the same serial and session and no zombie. unread(L, S) iff a row that is no trustd is at_slot, or in_session and unspared, or has an unread mark and either runs from under S (as named or canonical) or, off Linux with its executable unread, is in marked(L, None, ∅). may_signal(p, now) iff now is no zombie, has p's serial and ppid, neither it nor its parent reads as a trustd, and every link (pid_i, serial_i) of p.tie still holds serial_i under pid_{i+1}. send_with(P, sig) re-reads each p ∈ P in order and, for each that may_signal, returns (pid, serial) and signals it unless p.spawning; Live::keep records in the check's record what send_with(P, 0) reaches, signalling nothing. end_marked_with(procs, S, act): no listing → Unread; a first listing with none of the check's rows → Nothing iff it is whole and ¬unread, else Unread; then up to KILLS = 16 times list again (what a signal reached counting as the check's): no listing → Lives; none of the check's rows → Ended iff whole and ¬unread, else Lives; else keep and SIGKILL them and wait ≤ 1 s; Lives after the 16th. A sweep sends nothing but one SIGTERM round and SIGKILL rounds, never stops a process, and signals no row may_signal refuses or one caught mid-spawn. row(p) for this user's p > 1 other than this process (None for a zombie or one gone mid-read): marked = Some(true) for the mark's root or a kept (pid, serial), else, once its thread ran and its environment is not withheld, whether it carries CHECK_VAR = the mark's nonce, else None (a Linux leader whose other threads run included); at_slot and in_session only for a member of the root's session while the lane holds no root, at_slot when it works in, runs from or holds a file under S or that use cannot be read, in_session unless another process holds the root's pid, it has or had a controlling terminal, or the mark names a nonce and it reads without it; spawning iff it reads as forked (macOS: not exec'd) and its thread is not seen to have run. stat_kin(line, pid) is None unless the line's leading pid is pid and its fields through the start time are there, ppid, tty, thread count and start time as numbers; then ppid, the session (> 0, else None), ctty = tty ≠ 0, trustd = comm is 'trustd', serial = the start time, zombie iff the A one-shot POST is sent once and read by its exit (`--fail-with-body`: a 4xx is exit 22, `Refused`). A listing is read through `Link` pagination to its end; a page that advertises more and holds nothing is refused on every attempt. An asset is read by its id: the asset API's redirect is followed only onto the CDN origins (anything else is refused and never asked), anonymously. Reads and idempotent mutations are made at most 3 times; a definitive answer (a 4xx but 408/429, a refused redirect, a local refusal) once; none once the release has caught an interrupt. The post-cut reads (`release_steps::Real::github_read`) and the site sync's (`site::Real::github`) start each curl through their launch (`github::Launched` of `StepChildren`: a session of its own, the interrupt handed to it, noted while it runs, none once interrupted), so a SIGTERM sent to the release alone ends the curl and nothing more is asked; a launch's refusal to start one is a local refusal, never asked again.	src/github_tests.rs::a_draft_is_created_uploaded_published_listed_and_read_back_through_curl, src/github_tests.rs::the_token_rides_curls_stdin_and_never_its_argv_or_environment, src/github_tests.rs::a_write_without_the_token_is_refused_before_curl_starts, src/github_tests.rs::the_listing_is_read_whole_and_an_incomplete_page_is_refused, src/github_tests.rs::an_asset_redirect_off_the_cdn_is_refused_and_never_followed, src/github_tests.rs::a_hostile_curlrc_is_never_read, src/github_tests.rs::rows_are_jqs_tsv, src/github_tests.rs::a_launched_read_starts_each_curl_through_the_launch_and_none_once_interrupted, src/github_tests.rs::a_real_sigterm_reaches_a_post_cut_reads_curl_and_nothing_more_is_asked	curl child, loopback fake GitHub, thread-local fixture, signals and process groups	1,8
rel-site-hosting-deploy	invariant	site::hosting::deploy, site::hosting::rest::Client::token, site::hosting::rest::Client::call, site::hosting::rest::Origins::admit, site::hosting::key::Key::read, site::hosting::key::Key::assertion, site::hosting::Plan::of, site::Real::deploy	A deploy is, in order: one token request to the key's token_uri, admitted only when it is exactly Origins.token (https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token in production; anything else is Failed::Credential with no curl started), its RS256 JWT-bearer assertion (iss = client_email, scope = cloud-platform, aud = token_uri, exp = iat + 3600) signed by the key's PKCS#8 RSA key and sent as the body on curl's stdin; sites.versions.create with the plan's config; versions.populateFiles naming every planned path by the SHA-256 of its gzip member (aterm_png::gzip: MTIME 0, no name, OS 255; zlib level 9's search, each block the smallest of dynamic, fixed and stored, so a member is within 3% of gzip -9n), in batches of 1000; an upload of each distinct member whose hash Hosting asked for, and no other, to the uploadUrl, refused unless on Origins.upload; versions.patch status=FINALIZED; then sites.releases.create, the one call that changes what the live site serves. Every request is one /usr/bin/curl -q with the bearer on stdin as -H @-, the answer on stdout: the token and the assertion are never in an argument, the environment, a file of the client's scratch directory, or the log. A failure before releases.create is Failed::NotReleased naming the step and the version left unreleased (the live release untouched); 401/403, a refused key or none is Failed::Credential; a releases.create that went out and failed is Failed::Release (live state unknown), and one that never went out (curl refused or never started, or it never reached the endpoint: exit 1, 3, 5, 6, 7, 35, 60) is Failed::NotReleased naming the finalized version. The token endpoint's answers are said by their status and error fields only, never their body. The plan is public/ less hosting.ignore, sorted; a symbolic link, a non-UTF-8 name, or a JSON file holding a string private_key is refused. else Fix{c, its subject, diff-tree(c^, c)}. landed_since(F) is the set of commits reached from origin/main's tip, among those F does not reach, following every parent of a commit except a merge whose tree is its first parent's tree (content-neutral, as every record merge is), of which only the first parent; so a record merge's second parent (another line's hop) and anything reached only through it are not in it, and every commit of origin/main's first-parent chain since F and every side of a merge that changed the tree is. tree_has(r, p) = Ok(true) iff r's tree has a blob (or symlink) at exactly p, Ok(false) for no entry there or a tree there, COULD NOT RUN (Unread) for a commit this clone lacks or a tree git cannot list. order(fixes) is the duplicate-free fixes with each one after every ancestor of it among them, ties by committer time then sha, whatever the argument order. hop_tree(P, c) = `git --attr-source=<empty tree> -c core.attributesFile=/dev/null merge-tree --write-tree -X no-renames -X no-renormalize -X diff-algorithm=histogram --merge-base c^ P c` (P's tree with c's patch, as git apply makes it: a rename is a deletion and an addition), a function of the commits alone: the checkout's .gitattributes and a configured attributes file never reach it, and when merge.default is set, or $GIT_DIR/info/attributes or the system gitattributes give a path c moves a merge attribute, it is COULD NOT RUN (Steered) naming them. carry(L, fixes) = H1…Hn with parents(H1) = [h], parents(Hi) = [Hi-1], tree(Hi) = hop_tree(Hi-1, ci), message 'release line v{v} hop {k}: carries {ci} ({subject})', ci's author and committer with their dates, unsigned: the same commits on every rebuild, under any identity; Hi.paths = diff-tree(Hi-1, Hi); nothing is pushed. ### Proof dependency order

Easiest and highest-value first. Within a tier, specs are ordered by how few gaps block them, then by how many tests they retire. A pure core named in an effect spec (e.g. `turn_verdict` inside `rel-slice-drives-session`, `fence_liveness` inside `fence-reclaim-only-dead`, `durable_post_decision` inside `durable-post-refines-model`) is proved in its own earlier tier first, and the later spec composes it (gap 4).

## 3. Coverage

- 909 tests originally read (880 mapped to 372 specs; 29 not spec samples in §4). After subsequent deletions and additions, the crate currently has 1,114 live `#[test]` functions in the tree (`build.rs` removed; 10 non-spec tests remain in §4).
- Spec samples per file: src/apple.rs 10, src/buildplan.rs 18, src/bundle.rs 3, src/changelog.rs 10, src/channel.rs 15, src/clean.rs 3, src/dev_app.rs 12, src/dev_identity.rs 12, src/dmg.rs 1, src/gates.rs 56, src/linux_release.rs 9, src/machines.rs 10, src/machines/current_roster_tests.rs 4, src/provision.rs 20, src/publish.rs 48, src/release_steps.rs 21, src/sign.rs 7, src/site/diff.rs 1, src/site/engine.rs 4, src/site/hosting/files.rs 5, src/site/hosting/key.rs 2, src/site/hosting/rest.rs 3, src/site/hosting/tests.rs 14, src/site/linux.rs 5, src/site/mod.rs 4, src/site/notes.rs 3, src/site/pages.rs 7, src/site/releases.rs 11, src/site/sitemap.rs 2, src/site/toolchain.rs 5, src/site/world.rs 2, src/snapshot.rs 1, src/transforms.rs 4, src/trust_facts.rs 28, src/trust_lane.rs 103, src/trust_oblig.rs 4, src/verify.rs 8, tests/it/apple_tier.rs 45, tests/it/build_once.rs 9, tests/it/changelog_golden.rs 11, tests/it/channel_floor_model.rs 6, tests/it/channel_latest.rs 24, tests/it/claim_landing_model.rs 3, tests/it/durable_post_intent_model.rs 9, tests/it/handoff_policy_bundle.rs 2, tests/it/hooks.rs 12, tests/it/journal_prefix_model.rs 1, tests/it/ledger_race.rs 19, tests/it/machine_roster.rs 19, tests/it/plist_stamp.rs 10, tests/it/publisher_fence_liveness.rs 17, tests/it/publisher_fence_model.rs 5, tests/it/release_fixtures.rs 6, tests/it/release_flow.rs 64, tests/it/release_flow/qualify_model.rs 2, tests/it/release_flow/sign_model.rs 2, tests/it/release_lease.rs 9, tests/it/resume.rs 47, tests/it/signconf.rs 12, tests/it/site_sync.rs 26, tests/it/snapshot_take.rs 15, tests/it/trust_facts_model.rs 6.
- The biggest collapses (primary tests): `channel-head-safety` (12), `fence-reclaim-only-dead` (11), `rs-scan-release-page` (10), `plist-stamp-contract` (9).
- 369 specs retire at least one test as primary. 3 specs are only secondary for every test they cite (another, more specific spec retires each one) but each states a property of its own (a composed path or a second function), so it is still proved: `rel-x86-probe-record`, `rel-slice-drives-session`, `rl-lease-mutual-exclusion`.
- The release-flow invariants (`rf-*`) are stated over a `Backend` trait object and need gaps 2, 4, 8 and 11 at least; they cover the 64 spec-sample tests of `tests/it/release_flow.rs` but come last in practice.

## 4. Tests that are not spec samples

> Status 2026-10-16: deleted `src/changelog.rs::bounding_an_already_bounded_body_is_a_no_op`, `src/gates.rs::the_probe_source_still_carries_an_obligation` and `src/gates.rs::the_from_source_remedy_is_the_seed_first_recreator_then_a_seal`. `build.rs::rerun_and_dirty_inputs_are_one_source_of_truth` was not found (build.rs no longer exists; `SOURCE_INPUTS` moved to src/cutter.rs).
>
> Status 2026-10-17: `src/gates.rs::this_binary_carries_a_bounded_git_stamp` and `src/gates.rs::the_gate_knows_the_name_of_the_table_the_config_actually_carries` are deleted too, and the rows of these six are gone from the table below.
>
> Status 2026-10-28: `src/gates.rs::the_from_source_remedy_names_no_home_folder_and_is_atpkgs_command` is deleted; `gates.rs::trust_from_source!` and `aterm_toolchain::Toolchain::INSTALL_REMEDY` now expand the single `aterm_toolchain::trust_recreate_bootstrap_cmd!` macro.
>
> No test reads a `.rs` file of this crate as text: a source property is held by construction, and its test drives the construction. The release POST body is `ReleaseBody`, made only by `release_body_for_post` (`src/publish.rs::an_oversized_notes_file_on_disk_is_bounded_at_the_post`); `after_upload_post` runs the settle before it asks the probe (`tests/it/durable_post_intent_model.rs::what_an_upload_does_after_its_post_is_one_table`); both withdrawals end through `withdraw_tag_then_unlock` (`tests/it/durable_post_intent_model.rs::recovery_cleanup_selector_refines_the_historical_recovery_model`); the release build's environment is `release_build_command`'s (`src/buildplan.rs::the_release_build_enables_no_development_seam`); an asset is read by its id alone, through the CDN redirect `github::open_asset` holds to the CDN origins (`src/github_tests.rs::an_asset_redirect_off_the_cdn_is_refused_and_never_followed`).

Actions: **delete** (samples nothing, or duplicates a spec sample); **move-to-bench** (a guard over
committed repository data, source text or the build environment: it belongs in an `aterm-census`
guard row, a `pub doctor` check or the bench, not in a function spec).

| test | why | action |
|---|---|---|
| src/buildplan.rs::the_release_build_enables_no_development_seam | Repo-wide guard: walks every Cargo.toml for dev-seams, reads `[profile.release]` and `.cargo/config.toml`, and orders source text in `build_one`. Its argv half is `rel-cargo-build-args`. | move-to-bench |
| src/buildplan.rs::parser_reads_the_real_native_test_macho_section | Integration check that this build's linker embedded EXPECTED in `__aterm_upin`: a property of the build, not of the parser (`rel-thin-macho-pin-parser`). | move-to-bench |
| src/channel.rs::the_channel_is_exactly_the_one_clients_compiled_in | Binds the workspace Cargo.toml key to `aterm_update_core`'s build-script constants: repo state. | move-to-bench |
| src/gates.rs::the_checked_in_policy_passes_the_gate | Repo-state guard over handoff-policy.toml. | move-to-bench |
| src/linux_release.rs::the_worker_script_is_parsed_whole_before_it_runs | Pins a const shell script's shape and runs `bash -n` if `/bin/bash` exists (passing silently otherwise): an external-tool lint of a constant. | move-to-bench |
| src/site/notes.rs::the_changelog_page_is_the_reviewed_golden | Golden snapshot of rendered HTML; the renderer properties are `rel-site-render-markdown` and `rel-site-from-changelog`. | move-to-bench |
| src/site/notes.rs::the_release_bodies_page_is_the_reviewed_golden | Golden snapshot of `render_page`; its stable-tag sub-assertion is covered by `rel-site-stable`. | move-to-bench |
| tests/it/export_policy.rs::the_export_selects_no_gitlink_and_none_of_the_withheld_files | Asserts what this repository's publish/manifest.txt selects: a guard over committed data. | move-to-bench |
| tests/it/export_policy.rs::the_public_clone_command_reads_as_one_line_and_admits_only_a_public_version | Checks publish/config.sh's `CHECK_CMD_DEFAULT` and runs grep on it: a tree-content guard over a shell file. | move-to-bench |
| tests/it/plist_stamp.rs::the_shipped_bundle_requires_native_execution | Template half is a data-file guard; stamping half follows from `plist-stamp-contract`. | move-to-bench |

## 5. Implied specs that look wrong, or disagree with the code

1. **Resolved — units unified on `github_length`.** `src/changelog.rs::the_assembled_document_fits_what_github_accepts`
   and `src/publish.rs::an_oversized_notes_file_on_disk_is_bounded_at_the_post` now assert
   `github_length(doc) <= GITHUB_RELEASE_BODY_LIMIT` over em-dash-bearing text.
2. **Resolved — `release_body_post_failure_detail` passes `github_length`.** `create_channel_draft`
   now passes UTF-16 code units (`changelog::github_length(&notes)`) rather than UTF-8 byte length
   to `release_post_failure_detail`, so multi-byte bodies within the 125 000-character limit are
   reported `'WITHIN it'`.
3. **Resolved — `size_label` promotes rounded `1000 MB` to `"1.00 GB"`.** `site/releases.rs::size_label`
   now compares `m.round_ties_even() >= 1000.0` before formatting MB, so `n ∈ [999 500 000, 999 999 999]`
   yields `"1.00 GB"` and `the_size_is_the_finders_decimal_units` samples the boundary.
4. **Resolved — `newest_published_source` orders RFC 3339 timestamps by UTC instant.**
   `gates.rs::newest_published_source` now parses RFC 3339 (`Z`/`z` or `±HH:MM`, optional fractional
   nanoseconds) via `rfc3339_instant` before string tie-breaking, tested across mixed offsets.
5. **Resolved — `is_staging_dir_name` matches canonical `u64` staging names.** `bundle.rs::is_staging_dir_name`
   now requires `n.parse::<u64>().is_ok_and(|k| k.to_string() == n)`, rejecting leading zeros, signs and
   values above `u64::MAX`.
6. **Self-signed status suffix in `security find-identity`.** `dev_identity.rs::identity_hashes` matches
   `rest.starts_with("\"aterm dev\"")` (both quote delimiters included, so longer common names such as
   `"aterm dev 2"` do not match). Trailing macOS Keychain annotations after the closing quote — notably
   ` (CSSMERR_TP_NOT_TRUSTED)`, which macOS always prints on the self-signed `"aterm dev"` certificate —
   are intentionally accepted (`rel-dev-identity-hashes`).
7. **Resolved — case-insensitive digest match exercised.** `apple.rs::the_certificate_is_selected_by_digest_not_by_position`
   now uses mixed-case hex letters (`abcdef` vs `ABCDEF`, `FEDCBA` vs `fedcba`) in its SHA-1 fixtures.
8. **Resolved — `the_worker_script_is_parsed_whole_before_it_runs` reports `could_not_run` without `/bin/bash`.**
   When `/bin/bash` is absent, the test now calls `aterm_spec::host::could_not_run` and returns rather
   than passing silently.
9. **Resolved — `the_universal_cut_drives_a_session_on_its_x86_64_slice` uses `Duration::ZERO` on the silent stand-in.**
   The silent stand-in case now passes `Duration::ZERO` (an expired poll deadline) and asserts the
   `"opened no control socket"` error rather than sleeping 300 ms.
10. **Unreachable inputs.** `tests/it/resume.rs::first_incomplete_walks_the_step_order` feeds `done` lists
    with gaps or reordering that `Journal::save/load` make unreachable (`journal-roundtrip-valid`);
    the gap behaviour holds only for invalid journals.
11. **Resolved — `release.rs::estimates` documents its upper-median rule and is sampled by unit test.**
    `estimates` now documents `sorted[len / 2]` (upper median on even counts) over the last ≤ 5 valid
    runs, and `release::tests::estimates_takes_upper_median_of_last_five_and_formats_durations` samples
    `rf-estimates-fmt-dur`.
12. **Goldens as definitions.** `tests/it/site_sync.rs::a_release_sync_rewrites_rebuilds_commits_pushes_deploys_and_verifies`
    and `release_bodies_render_releases_when_the_public_tree_has_no_changelog` compare /releases to
    golden HTML and pin commit-message prose; `ss-pages-rewrite` takes the renderer as the definition.
13. **Partial resume coverage.** `tests/it/release_flow.rs::a_run_stopped_at_any_step_resumes_there_and_repeats_nothing_done`
    checks only the calls the first run made before its stop step; other tests sample the rest of
    `rf-resume-no-repeat`.
14. **Model-only negative controls.** The `tests/it/trust_facts_model.rs` negative controls
    (`the_pre_facts_receipt_reader_is_refused`, `the_unchecked_reuse_reader_is_refused`,
    `the_retired_floor_excuses_are_refused`) check that the model refuses a hand-built state, which
    holds whatever the code does (`tf-model-safety`); only their trailing `assemble()`/`judge()` calls
    sample production (`judge-floor-rule`).
15. **Overlapping triage specs.** `rel-fresh-cut-triage` (one arm) is subsumed by
    `rs-older-format-triage` (the whole table); prove the latter and retire both tests with it.
16. **Literal `#[test]` in strings.** `src/transforms.rs` carries a `"#[test]"` inside a string
    literal (a fixture), so a naive count of the crate's tests is one too many.

## 6. The three specs I would prove first (easiest, high value)

1. **`ledger-next-build`** (gap 3 only), with `exact-delete-absence` (seal only) as the warm-up.
   `next_build(tail, now) = max(now, tail + 1)` with a floor check is two u64 comparisons; it is an
   `omega`-class proof once E6 admits `max`. It is the arithmetic core of "a build number is never
   burned or reused" (`ledger-claim-cas-safety`, `rf-no-build-number-burned`, 11 tests between them)
   and retires its test outright. `rs-effective-min-build` and `rs-monotonic` (gaps 1, 3, scalar
   `Option<u64>`) follow with the same tactics.
2. **`rel-durable-post-decision`** (gaps 1, 3). Two `const fn`s from `bool`/`Option<bool>` to fieldless
   enums: an exhaustive case split. It is the pure decision under `durable-post-refines-model`
   (5 tests) — the "an issued intent never re-POSTs" safety rule — so proving it first lets the
   refinement bind to a certified function instead of an observed one. `lane-outcome-exit`,
   `rel-roster-duty`, `resume-attribution-agrees` and `successor-cut-options` (gap 1 only) are the
   same shape and nearly free alongside it.
3. **`rel-staging-dir-names`** (gaps 1, 3, 5, 7). `staging_dir_name` is injective and
   `is_staging_dir_name` recognises exactly the names it produces (`∃k: u64`), which guards what
   the pruner may delete.

Runner-up: the other pure string parsers blocked only by gaps 1/3/5/7 (`rs-claim-provenance`;
`rel-anon-probe-refusal`; `parse-prerelease-flag`). They wait on gap 1's `&str` carrier, which
nearly every spec in this crate needs (§1 histogram).
